Full answer
-----------

Answer: $1,500,000

Why

| Program | Key rules for this partnership | Max for 4 partners | |---|---|---| | MASC (Doc 2) | • AGI >$900k is allowed if ≥75% is farm income. Here 70% is crops; because that base is ≥66.66%, the remaining 30% (seed sales = “provision of production inputs”) is added, making farm income 100% → partner is eligible.<br>• Payment cap: $125,000 per person/legal entity (joint ventures/general partnerships are excluded from the entity‑level cap, but payments are attributed to each partner and each partner is subject to the $125k limit).<br>• No partner is ineligible, so no reduction applies. | 4 × $125,000 = $500,000 | | ASCF (Doc 7) | • Same AGI/$900k rule applies via 7 CFR part 1400 Subpart F (same 66.66%/75% farm‑income test), so the partner is eligible here too.<br>• Payment cap: $250,000 per person, legal entity, or member of a joint venture/general partnership (§1414.109(a)). | 4 × $250,000 = $1,000,000 |

Because all four partners remain eligible and none trigger an ownership‑share reduction, the partnership can receive the full per‑partner caps in both programs.

Combined maximum: $500,000 + $1,000,000 = $1,500,000

We need examine MASC rules for AGI limitation and payment limitation and attribution to partners.

- Definitions include "Average adjusted gross farm income" and "Average AGI" for base period 2021-2023.

- Under "Average AGI Limitation and Payment Limitation":

"A person or legal entity, other than a joint venture or general partnership, will not be eligible to receive, directly or indirectly, a MASC payment if the average adjusted gross income of the person or legal entity exceeds $900,000 for 2021, 2022, and 2023, unless the person or legal entity's average adjusted gross farm income is at least 75 percent of their average AGI."

Then "A person or legal entity, other than a joint venture or general partnership, cannot receive, directly or indirectly, more than $125,000 in MASC payments."

Then attribution rules: "A payment made to a legal entity will be attributed to those members who have a direct or indirect ownership interest in the legal entity, unless the payment of the legal entity has been reduced by the proportionate ownership interest of the member due to that member's ineligibility."

Then tracking through four levels of ownership.

From Document 2, definition of "Income derived from farming, ranching, and forestry operations" includes:
(1) production of crops...
...
(6) processing, packing, storing, transportation...
(7) feeding...
(8) payments of benefits...
(9) sale of land used for agricultural purposes
(10) benefits from federal programs...
(11) income reported on IRS Schedule F...
(12) wages or dividends received from closely held corporation... more than 50 percent gross receipts derived from farming...
(13) any other activity related to farming, ranching, or forestry...

Also there is specific mention: "The provision of production inputs and production services to farmers, ranchers, foresters, and farm operations" is included in average adjusted gross farm income if average AGI derived from items 1-12 is at least 66.66 percent.

Specifically, the definition of "Average adjusted gross farm income" says:
"If the resulting average adjusted gross farm income derived from items 1 through 12... is at least 66.66 percent of the average AGI of the person or legal entity, then the average adjusted gross farm income may also take into consideration income or benefits derived from the following:
(1) The sale, trade, or other disposition of equipment to conduct farm, ranch, or forestry operations; and
(2) The provision of production inputs and production services to farmers, ranchers, foresters, and farm operations."

So for the partner, 70% is from crops (item 1). The remaining 30% is from selling seed to farmers, which is provision of production inputs. Since 70% >= 66.66%, the 30% can also be included in farm income. Thus total farm income is 100%, which is >=75% of AGI. Therefore this partner is NOT ineligible due to AGI; they meet the exception (farm income at least 75%). Second level of ownership—any payment made to a first-level legal entity that is owned in whole or in part by another legal entity (referred to as a second-level legal entity) will be attributed to the second-level legal entity in proportion to the ownership of the second-level legal entity in the first-level legal entity; if the second-level legal entity is owned in whole or in part by a person, the amount of the payment made to the first-level legal entity will be attributed to the person in the amount that represents the indirect ownership in the first-level legal entity by the person;
Third and fourth levels of ownership—except as provided in the second level of ownership bullet above and in the fourth level of ownership bullet below, any payments made to a legal entity at the third and fourth levels of ownership will be attributed in the same manner as specified in the second level of ownership bullet above; and
Fourth-level of ownership—if the fourth level of ownership is that of a legal entity and not that of a person, a reduction in payment will be applied to the first-level or payment legal entity in the amount that represents the indirect ownership in the first level or payment legal entity by the fourth-level legal entity."

"Payments made directly or indirectly to a person who is a minor child will be combined with the earnings of the minor's parent or legal guardian.

Full answer
-----------

Answer: $1,500,000

Why:

1. The high-AGI partner is still eligible for both programs

- MASC (Doc 2): A person with average AGI >$900,000 is ineligible unless average adjusted gross farm income is ≥75% of AGI.
- 70% is from crop production (counts toward farm income).
- Because that 70% already exceeds the 66.66% threshold, the remaining 30% from selling seed (production inputs) to farmers can also be included.
- Total farm income = 100% of AGI → well above the 75% exception. So this partner is not disqualified.
- ASCF (Doc 7): Uses 7 CFR part 1400, which contains the same 75%-farm-income exception, so he remains eligible here too.

1. MASC payment limit for a general partnership

- The $125,000 cap applies to each person (not to the partnership itself), and payments to partnerships are attributed to the partners by ownership share (Doc 2, “Attribution of payments…”).
- With 4 partners and all eligible, the partnership can receive up to:

4 × $125,000 = $500,000 in MASC.

1. ASCF payment limit

- The rule caps each “person, legal entity, or member of a … general partnership” at $250,000 (Doc 7, §1414.109(a)).
- With 4 eligible partners:

4 × $250,000 = $1,000,000 in ASCF.

1. Combined maximum

- $500,000 (MASC) + $1,000,000 (ASCF) = $1,500,000.

So, because the partner meets the farm-income exception and all four partners are eligible, the most the partnership could receive combined is $1,500,000.

| Program | Key rules that control the partnership’s total | |---|---| | MASC (Document 2) | • Payment limit: $125,000 per person/legal entity; general partnerships are excluded from the direct entity cap, but payments are attributed to each partner according to ownership share (Sec. “Payment Limitation” and attribution rules). <br>• AGI rule: A person is ineligible if average AGI > $900k unless average adjusted gross farm income is ≥ 75 % of AGI (Sec. “Average AGI Limitation”). <br>• Farm‑income definition: Income from crops is in items 1‑12. If that portion is ≥ 66.66 % of AGI, then income from selling production inputs (seed) to farmers can also be counted (Sec. “Average adjusted gross farm income”). Here the partner has 70 % crop income (> 66.66 %), so the 30 % seed‑sale income is included. Total farm income = 100 % of AGI, which is ≥ 75 %. Therefore the partner is still eligible and there is no reduction for his share. <br>• With 4 equal partners, the partnership can absorb four full individual caps: 4 × $125,000 = $500,000. | | ASCF (Document 7) | • Payment limit: $250,000 per person, legal entity, or member of a joint venture/general partnership (Sec. 1414.109(a)). <br>• AGI eligibility uses 7 CFR part 1400, Subpart F, which contains the same 75 % farm‑income exception (Sec. 1414.109(b)). Because the partner’s farm income is 100 %, he is eligible here too. <br>• So four partners can each reach the cap: 4 × $250,000 = $1,000,000. |

Combined maximum

- MASC: $500,000
- ASCF: $1,000,000
- Total: $1,500,000

Because the partner with AGI over $900,000 still meets the 75 % farm‑income exception (70 % crops + 30 % seed‑sales = 100 %), he is not ineligible, so no proportionate reduction applies to the partnership’s payments in either program.

Full answer
-----------

Answer: $1,500,000

Why

1. The partner is still eligible

- Both MASC (Doc 2) and ASCF (Doc 7, applying 7 CFR Part 1400) bar payments only when average AGI exceeds $900,000 unless average farm income is ≥ 75 % of AGI.
- For MASC, “farm income” first counts items 1‑12. This partner’s crop income alone is 70 % of AGI, which exceeds the 66.66 % gate. So his seed‑sales income (production inputs to farmers) can also be counted (Doc 2, “Average adjusted gross farm income” definition).
- 70 % + 30 % = 100 % farm income > 75 %, so he meets the exception and is not ineligible. No share reduction applies.

1. General‑partnership treatment

- MASC and ASCF both exclude joint ventures/general partnerships from the entity‑level caps; instead payments are attributed to each partner (Doc 2, “Attribution of payments…”; Doc 7, § VI).
- Each partner then faces the individual caps:
- MASC: $125,000 per person (Doc 2, “Payment Limitation”).
- ASCF: $250,000 per person (Doc 7, § VI).

1. Maximum for four partners

- All four are eligible, so each can reach his/her cap:
- Per partner: $125,000 (MASC) + $250,000 (ASCF) = $375,000
- Partnership total: 4 × $375,000 = $1,500,000

So the most the four‑partner general partnership could receive from both programs combined is $1,500,000.

Answer: $1,500,000

Why that partner is still eligible

- For both programs, a producer with average AGI over $900,000 is ineligible unless at least 75% of that AGI is “farm income.”
- Under MASC, “income derived from farming, ranching, and forestry operations” includes crop sales (items 1‑12). If those alone reach 66.66% of AGI, then income from selling production inputs (e.g., seed) can also be counted.
- This partner gets 70% from crops (already >66.66%), so the remaining 30% from seed sales also counts. His farm income is 100% of AGI, well above the 75% threshold. So he is not disqualified and no share is forfeited.

How the payment caps apply to a 4‑partner general partnership

| Program | Cap rule (from the documents) | Per‑partner cap | Partnership max (4 partners) | |---|---|---|---| | MASC | $125,000 per “person or legal entity” (joint ventures and general partnerships are addressed by attributing payments to members) | $125,000 | 4 × $125,000 = $500,000 | | ASCF | $250,000 per “person, legal entity, or member of a joint venture or general partnership” (§1414.109(a)) | $250,000 | 4 × $250,000 = $1,000,000 |

Because the high‑AGI partner remains eligible, there is no proportional reduction (e.g., no 25% cut).

Combined maximum

- MASC: $500,000
- ASCF: $1,000,000
- Total: $1,500,000

Full answer
-----------

The maximum combined payment from MASC and ASCF to a four-partner general partnership is $1,500,000, as the specified partner remains eligible for both programs despite an average AGI exceeding $900,000.

Reasoning:

1. MASC Payment Limitation (Document 2):

- "A person or legal entity, other than a joint venture or general partnership, cannot receive, directly or indirectly, more than $125,000 in MASC payments."
- Payments to a general partnership are attributed to its partners (persons). Each partner is subject to the $125,000 indirect payment limit.
- For a four-partner general partnership with equal ownership, the maximum MASC payment to the partnership is $125,000 × 4 = $500,000.

1. ASCF Payment Limitation (Document 7):

- "A person, legal entity, or member of a joint venture or general partnership... cannot receive, directly or indirectly, more than $250,000 under this subpart."
- Explicitly includes members of general partnerships. Each partner is subject to the $250,000 limit.
- For four partners, maximum ASCF payment to the partnership is $250,000 × 4 = $1,000,000.

1. AGI Eligibility Analysis for the Specified Partner:

- Partner has average AGI > $900,000, with 70% from crop production (qualifying as "production of crops" under Item 1 of farm income definition) and 30% from selling seed to farmers (qualifying as "provision of production inputs" under the farm income expansion rule).
- MASC rules (Document 2) state farm income includes Items 1–12 plus Items (1)–(2) if Items 1–12 income ≥ 66.66% of AGI. Here, 70% ≥ 66.66%, so seed income (30%) counts toward farm income.
- Total farm income = 100% of AGI, which exceeds the 75% threshold for the AGI exception ("average adjusted gross farm income is at least 75 percent of their average AGI").
- ASCF adopts 7 CFR Part 1400 Subpart F for AGI determinations (Document 7), which uses the same farm income exception as MASC (consistent with MASC's statement that its limits align with "other FSA programs").
- Conclusion: The partner is eligible for both programs; no payment reduction applies.

1. Combined Maximum:

- MASC max: $500,000
- ASCF max: $1,000,000
- Total: $1,500,000

Second level of ownership--any payment made to a first-level legal entity that is owned in whole or in part by another legal entity (referred to as a second-level legal entity) will be attributed to the second-level legal entity in proportion to the ownership of the second-level legal entity in the first-level legal entity; if the second-level legal entity is owned in whole or in part by a person, the amount of the payment made to the first-level legal entity will be attributed to the person in the amount that represents the indirect ownership in the first-level legal entity by the person;

Third and fourth levels of ownership--except as provided in the second level of ownership bullet above and in the fourth level of ownership bullet below, any payments made to a legal entity at the third and fourth levels of ownership will be attributed in the same manner as specified in the second level of ownership bullet above; and

Fourth-level of ownership--if the fourth level of ownership is that of a legal entity and not that of a person, a reduction in payment will be applied to the first-level or payment legal entity in the amount that represents the indirect ownership in the first level or payment legal entity by the fourth-level legal entity."

Maximum Combined MASC + ASCF Payment

Step 1: Determine Eligibility of the Partner with AGI > $900,000

Both programs apply an AGI limitation (average AGI of 2021–2023 tax years cannot exceed $900,000), unless the person's average adjusted gross farm income is at least 75% of their average AGI.

Applying the farm income test (per MASC's detailed definitions, incorporated via 7 CFR 1400):

| Income source | % of AGI | Farm income category | |---|---|---| | Crops | 70% | Items 1–12 (production of crops) | | Selling seed to farmers | 30% | Secondary category: "provision of production inputs" |

Threshold check: 70% (items 1–12) > 66.66% → the secondary category (production inputs like seed sales) can be included.

Total farm income = 70% + 30% = 100% of AGI, which is ≥ 75%.

✅ The partner qualifies for the exception and is eligible for both programs.

Step 2: Payment Limitations

| Program | Limit per person | General Partnership treatment | Max per partner | 4 partners | |---|---|---|---|---| | MASC | $125,000 | GP exempt; payments attributed to partners | $125,000 | $500,000 | | ASCF | $250,000 | GP exempt; payments attributed to partners | $250,000 | $1,000,000 |

Step 3: Combined Maximum

$$4 \times (\$125{,}000 + \$250{,}000) = 4 \times \$375{,}000 = \boxed{\$1{,}500{,}000}$$

All four partners are eligible (the AGI-limited partner qualifies through the 75% farm income exception), yielding a combined maximum payment of $1,500,000.

Full answer

Maximum Combined MASC + ASCF Payments for a Four-Partner General Partnership

Partner Eligibility Analysis

The partner with average AGI exceeding $900,000 has 70% from crops and 30% from selling seed to farmers.

MASC (Doc 2): The AGI limitation applies to persons "other than a joint venture or general partnership," so it reaches the individual partners. However, MASC provides an exception if average adjusted gross farm income ≥ 75% of average AGI. Here:

- Crops (item 1 of farm income definition) = 70% of AGI → exceeds the 66.66% threshold to conditionally include additional farm-related income
- Selling seed to farmers qualifies as "provision of production inputs...to farmers" (Production inputs = "material to conduct farming operations, such as seeds, chemicals...")
- Total farm income = 70% + 30% = 100% ≥ 75% → partner is eligible for MASC

ASCF (Doc 7): The AGI limitation is a flat $900,000 cutoff with no farm income exception: "a person or legal entity, other than a joint venture or general partnership, is ineligible... if average adjusted gross income... exceeds $900,000." → This partner is ineligible for ASCF.

Payment Limits (Both programs exempt GPs from entity-level limits; limits apply per partner)

| | Eligible Partners | Limit per Person | Max Payment | |---|---|---|---| | MASC ($125,000/person) | 4 (all eligible) | $125,000 | $500,000 | | ASCF ($250,000/person) | 3 (one ineligible) | $250,000 | $750,000 |

Under 7 CFR 1400 attribution rules, the ineligible partner's ASCF share is reduced to zero and the GP's payment is reduced proportionally, capping at 3 × $250,000.

Combined Maximum: $500,000 + $750,000 = $1,250,000

Full answer

Combined MASC + ASCF Maximum Payment Calculation

Partner Eligibility (AGI > $900,000)

The partner earns 70% from crops and 30% from selling seed to farmers. Under MASC's definition of average adjusted gross farm income:

1. Items 1–12 (core farm income) = 70% of AGI (crops = Item 1)
2. Test: 70% ≥ 66.66%? Yes → the additional income category applies
3. "Selling seed to farmers" qualifies as "provision of production inputs to farmers"
4. Total average adjusted gross farm income = 100% of AGI
5. AGI limitation test: 100% ≥ 75%? Yes → Partner is eligible despite AGI > $900,000

This same determination applies to ASCF, which incorporates 7 CFR 1400, Subpart F (including the 75% farm-income exception).

MASC Maximum

| Rule | Limit | |---|---| | Payment cap per person/legal entity (excluding JVs & GPs) | $125,000 | | General partnership itself | Exempt from cap | | Each partner (a person, limited "directly or indirectly") | $125,000 | | 4 partners × $125,000 | $500,000 |

ASCF Maximum

| Rule | Limit | |---|---| | Payment cap per person, legal entity, or member of a JV/GP | $250,000 | | Each partner | $250,000 | | 4 partners × $250,000 | $1,000,000 |

Combined Maximum

$$\boxed{\$500{,}000 + \$1{,}000{,}000 = \$1{,}500{,}000}$$

All four partners—including the one with AGI exceeding $900,000—remain eligible because their average adjusted gross farm income (100% of AGI, after including seed sales as production inputs) exceeds the 75% threshold in both programs.

The question is whether "selling seed to farmers" counts as "income derived from farming, ranching, and forestry operations."

Looking at the definition of "income derived from farming, ranching, and forestry operations" in Document 2:
- (1) Production of crops and unfinished raw forestry products
- (2) Production of livestock, aquaculture products used for food, honeybees, and products derived from livestock
- ...
- (12) Wages or dividends received from a closely held corporation, an Interest Charge Domestic International Sales Corporation (IC-DISC), or legal entity comprised entirely of family members when more than 50 percent of the legal entity's gross receipts for each tax year are derived from farming, ranching, and forestry activities as defined in this document

Also, the definition of "Average adjusted gross farm income" says:
"If the resulting average adjusted gross farm income derived from items 1 through 12 of the definition of income derived from farming, ranching, and forestry operations is at least 66.66 percent of the average AGI of the person or legal entity, then the average adjusted gross farm income may also take into consideration income or benefits derived from the following:
(1) The sale, trade, or other disposition of equipment to conduct farm, ranch, or forestry operations; and
(2) The provision of production inputs and production services to farmers, ranchers, foresters, and farm operations."

So "selling seed to farmers" is "the provision of production inputs...to farmers" which is item (2) of the additional categories, not items 1-12 of the core definition.

The core farming income (items 1-12) includes crops (item 1). Selling seed to farmers is NOT in items 1-12. It falls under the additional category that can only be included if items 1-12 alone provide at least 66.66% of average AGI.

Since 70% of income is from crops (items 1-12), that's 70% which exceeds 66.66%. So the average adjusted gross farm income CAN also include the seed selling income (provision of production inputs to farmers).

Maximum Combined MASC + ASCF Payments for a Four-Partner General Partnership

Step 1: Determine Whether the High-AGI Partner Is Eligible

The partner has AGI exceeding $900,000 with:

- 70% from crops → "Production of crops" (income item 1 through 12)
- 30% from selling seed to farmers → "The provision of production inputs" (seeds are explicitly listed as production inputs)

The two-step farm income test:

1. Items 1–12 income = 70% ≥ 66.66% ✓ → production-input income can be included
2. Total farm income = 70% + 30% = 100% ≥ 75% ✓ → partner qualifies for the farm income exception

All four partners are therefore eligible under both programs.

Step 2: MASC Payment Limitation

- MASC caps payments at $125,000 per person (general partnerships themselves are exempt from the cap, but each individual partner—as a "person"—is subject to it).
- 4 partners × $125,000 = $500,000

Step 3: ASCF Payment Limitation

- ASCF caps payments at $250,000 per person or legal entity, or member of a joint venture or general partnership.
- 4 partners × $250,000 = $1,000,000

Combined Maximum

| Program | Per-Partner Cap | Partners | Total | |---------|----------------|----------|-------| | MASC | $125,000 | 4 | $500,000 | | ASCF | $250,000 | 4 | $1,000,000 |

$1,500,000

Maximum Combined MASC + ASCF Payment for the Four-Partner General Partnership

Step 1: Determine Eligibility of the Partner with AGI Over $900,000

This partner has 70% from crops and 30% from selling seed to farmers. Both programs apply the average AGI limitation of $900,000 with a farm-income exception requiring average adjusted gross farm income to be at least 75% of average AGI.

Is seed sales income farm income? Under the MASC income definition (Document 2), items 1–12 cover farming income. Selling seeds to farmers is not in items 1–12 directly. However, the extended definition applies when items 1–12 already reach ≥ 66.66% of average AGI. At that threshold, income from "provision of production inputs…to farmers" may also be counted — and seeds are explicitly listed as production inputs.

- Items 1–12 (crops, item 1): 70% ≥ 66.66% ✅
- Seeds (production inputs) added: +30%
- Total adjusted gross farm income = 100% ≥ 75% ✅

→ The high-AGI partner is eligible under both programs. All four partners qualify.

Step 2: Apply Payment Limitations

Payments to a general partnership (a legal entity) are attributed to individual partners, each of whom is a "person" subject to the per-person payment limits. The general partnership itself is exempt from the limit ("other than a joint venture or general partnership"), but attribution ensures the partner-level limits bind.

| Program | Per-Person Limit | Partners | Maximum for GP | |---------|-----------------|----------|----------------| | MASC (Doc. 2) | $125,000 | 4 | $500,000 | | ASCF (Doc. 7) | $250,000 | 4 | $1,000,000 |

Result

$$\boxed{\$500{,}000 + \$1{,}000{,}000 = \$1{,}500{,}000}$$

The most the partnership could receive in combined MASC and ASCF payments is $1,500,000.

Full answer
-----------

Maximum Combined MASC + ASCF Payments for a Four-Partner General Partnership

Step 1: Determine Eligibility of the Partner with AGI > $900,000

Both MASC and ASCF use the $900,000 average AGI limitation (2021–2023 tax years), with an exception if average adjusted gross farm income ≥ 75% of average AGI.

Two-step farm income test (from MASC definitions):

| Step | Calculation | Result | |------|-----------|--------| | Items 1–12 (e.g., "production of crops") | 70% of AGI | 70% ≥ 66.66% ✅ | | Additional items (since step 1 passes) | +30% ("provision of production inputs…to farmers" = selling seed) | — | | Total farm income | 70% + 30% = 100% | 100% ≥ 75% ✅ |

Selling seed qualifies as "provision of production inputs…to farmers" (seed is defined as a production input). Since crop income alone (70%) already exceeds the 66.66% threshold for items 1–12, the seed income can be added.

Result: The high-AGI partner IS eligible for both programs. All four partners are eligible.

Step 2: Payment Limitations

MASC (Document 2): A general partnership is excluded from the $125,000 entity cap; instead, each partner is limited to $125,000.

> 4 partners × $125,000 = $500,000

ASCF (Document 7): Each person, legal entity, or member of a general partnership is limited to $250,000.

> 4 partners × $250,000 = $1,000,000

Step 3: Combined Maximum

$$\$500{,}000 + \$1{,}000{,}000 = \boxed{\$1{,}500{,}000}$$

Full answer

MASC Payment Calculation

Based on the provided documents, ASCF is not addressed in any of the input documents, so only the MASC analysis can be provided.

Step 1: AGI Eligibility of the High-AGI Partner

The AGI limitation states a person (not a JV or GP) is ineligible if average AGI exceeds $900,000 unless average adjusted gross farm income is at least 75% of average AGI.

- 70% from crops (production of crops) → falls under items 1–12 of the farming income definition. Since 70% ≥ 66.66%, the threshold to include additional categories is met.
- 30% from selling seed to farmers → seed is a production input (explicitly defined as such), and "the provision of production inputs…to farmers" is an additional farming income category available after the 66.66% threshold is satisfied.

Total farm income = 70% + 30% = 100% of AGI ≥ 75% → This partner is eligible despite AGI exceeding $900,000.

Step 2: Partnership-Level Exemptions

The MASC rules state that a general partnership is exempt from both the $900,000 AGI limitation and the $125,000 payment limitation at the entity level.

Step 3: Attribution and Individual Limits

However, payments to a legal entity are attributed to its members. Each person (partner) has a $125,000 payment limitation. With four partners, all eligible:

> 4 × $125,000 = $500,000

Answer

The maximum combined payment from the documented programs is $500,000 (MASC only, since ASCF is not addressed in the provided documents).

Maximum MASC Payment Analysis

General Partnership Structure

Under the MASC program (Document 2), both the $125,000 payment limitation and the $900,000 AGI limitation explicitly exclude general partnerships at the entity level. Instead, these limits are applied at the individual partner level, with payments attributed through four levels of ownership.

AGI Eligibility of the High-AGI Partner

One partner has average AGI exceeding $900,000, with:

- 70% from crops → qualifies as "income derived from farming" (items 1–12)
- 30% from selling seed to farmers → qualifies under the extended items: "provision of production inputs and production services to farmers"

Two-tier AGI test:

| Step | Test | Result | |------|------|--------| | 1 | Is farm income from items 1–12 ≥ 66.66% of AGI? | 70% ≥ 66.66% ✅ | | 2 | Extended items may now be included → total farm income = 70% + 30% = 100% | ✅ | | 3 | Exception: Is average adjusted gross farm income ≥ 75% of AGI? | 100% ≥ 75% ✅ |

This partner passes the AGI test and remains eligible. All four partners are eligible.

MASC Maximum

$$4 \text{ eligible partners} \times \$125{,}000 = \$500{,}000$$

Regarding ASCF

The acronym "ASCF" does not appear in any of the provided input documents. Based solely on the documents provided, the maximum combined payment from MASC alone is $500,000. If ASCF refers to another program not covered in these documents, additional information would be needed to calculate a combined total.

Full answer

Maximum Combined MASC and ASCF Payment to a Four-Partner General Partnership

ASCF Program Analysis (Document 6)

Payment Limitation (§ 1414.109(a)): Each member of a general partnership may receive no more than $250,000 under the ASCF Program.

AGI Limitation (§ 1414.109(b)): A person with average AGI exceeding $900,000 (averaged over 2021–2023 tax years) is ineligible to receive benefits, directly or indirectly. Under 7 CFR part 1400, subpart F, there is an exception if 75% or more of total AGI comes from farming, ranching, or forestry.

Applying the AGI test to the ineligible partner:

- AGI exceeds $900,000 ✓
- 70% from crops (farming income) + 30% from selling seed to farmers (commercial/retail activity, not farming income)
- 70% < 75% → farming exception does not apply → partner is ineligible

ASCF maximum:

- Partner 1 (AGI > $900K): $0 (ineligible)
- Partners 2–4 (3 eligible members): 3 × $250,000 = $750,000

MASC Program

MASC is not explicitly detailed in the provided documents. Assuming it follows the same standard USDA payment limitation framework referenced in 7 CFR part 1400 ($250,000 per member; $900,000 AGI limit with the same 75% farming exception), the same analysis applies:

- 3 eligible partners × $250,000 = $750,000

Combined Maximum

| Program | Eligible Partners | Limit per Member | Maximum | |---------|:-:|:-:|:-:| | ASCF | 3 of 4 | $250,000 | $750,000 | | MASC | 3 of 4 | $250,000 | $750,000 | | Combined | | | $1,500,000 |

Answer: $1,500,000

The partner with AGI exceeding $900,000 is ineligible for both programs because only 70% of their income derives from farming (crops), falling short of the 75% farming-income exception. The remaining three partners can each receive up to $250,000 per program, yielding a combined maximum of $1,500,000.

Full answer
-----------

Analysis of Maximum Combined Payments

ASCF Program (Document 6)

Payment Limitation: Under 7 CFR § 1414.109(a), each member of a general partnership is limited to $250,000 in ASCF payments. The partnership entity itself is not separately capped.

AGI Eligibility (7 CFR Part 1400, Subpart F): Under § 1414.109(b), a person with average AGI exceeding $900,000 (averaged over 2021–2023 tax years) is ineligible—unless at least 75% of their average AGI is derived from farming, ranching, or forestry activities. The ASCF Program applies 7 CFR Part 1400 as in effect for program year 2025 (except Subparts C, E, and G).

Application to the partner with AGI > $900,000:

- 70% from crops (farm income) + 30% from selling seed (non-farm trade income)
- 70% < 75% → the farm-income exception does not apply
- This partner is ineligible for benefits, directly or indirectly

Result for ASCF:

- 3 eligible partners × $250,000 = $750,000 maximum from ASCF

MASC Program

The MASC program is not addressed anywhere in the provided documents, so no determination can be made about its payment limitations or AGI eligibility rules from the input materials.

Combined Maximum

Based solely on the provided documents, the ASCF Program could pay at most $750,000 to the general partnership (the one partner exceeding the $900,000 AGI threshold with only 70% farm income is ineligible). The MASC portion cannot be calculated from the documents given. If MASC carries analogous $250,000 per-person limits and the same AGI rules, the combined theoretical maximum would be $1,500,000, but this cannot be verified from the input materials.

- Each partner's share = X/4 (assuming equal shares)
- Ineligible partner's share = X/4 → not paid
- Eligible partners' shares = 3X/4, but each capped at $250,000

Maximum: 3 × $250,000 = $750,000 from ASCF

Now about the "70 percent from crops and the rest from selling seed" - I don't think this changes anything in the ASCF context. The AGI test is based on total AGI regardless of source.

But wait, could the "selling seed to farmers" be relevant to the ASCF eligibility? The ASCF is for specialty crop producers. Selling seed might not count as specialty crop production. But the AGI test is about total AGI, not just farm AGI.

Hmm, but actually, looking at 7 CFR part 1400, subpart F, the AGI limitation is based on "average adjusted gross income" which is typically the three-year average of total AGI as reported on tax returns. It doesn't distinguish between farm and non-farm income.

So the fact that 70% is from crops and 30% from seed sales doesn't change the AGI calculation. The partner's AGI exceeds $900,000 regardless.

OK so my answer for ASCF is $750,000.

For MASC - I cannot determine from the documents provided.

Actually, wait. I just want to double-check one thing. The payment limitation in the preamble says:

"The total amount of ASCF Program payments received, directly or indirectly, by a person or legal entity (except a joint venture or general partnership) may not exceed $250,000."

Wait, this says the $250,000 limit applies to a "person or legal entity (except a joint venture or general partnership)." So the $250,000 limit does NOT apply to the general partnership itself? It applies to each person/member?

Then the regulation says:

"1414.109(a) A person, legal entity, or member of a joint venture or general partnership, as determined in 7 CFR part 1400 in effect for program year 2025 on June 1, 2026, cannot receive, directly or indirectly, more than $250,000 under this subpart."

So for a general partnership, the $250,000 limit applies per member (each partner). The partnership itself isn't capped at $250,000; each partner is capped at $250,000.

So with 4 partners (3 eligible), max = 3 × $250,000 = $750,000.

Hmm, but maybe I'm wrong about the partner being ineligible. Let me re-read the AGI limitation.

The preamble: "a person or legal entity, other than a joint venture or general partnership, is ineligible for ASCF Program payments, directly or indirectly, if the person's or legal entity's average adjusted gross income, using the average of the adjusted gross incomes for the 2021, 2022, and 2023 tax years, exceeds $900,000."

This says "a person or legal entity" is ineligible if their AGI exceeds $900,000. The "other than a joint venture or general partnership" phrase means the partnership itself is exempt, but individual partners (who are "persons") are still subject to the test.

So partner with AGI > $900,000 is ineligible. Other 3 partners are eligible (assuming their AGI is ≤ $900,000).

Max from ASCF = 3 × $250,000 = $750,000.
